STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/K/Kawasaki_Ryo/
> COMMENT: Ryo Kawasaki's own comments are denoted (RK).
>          "When the first time Commodore 64 computer hit the market in 1983, I
>          rushed to buy it by paying $600 at some electronic store on 45th
>          street in Manhattan. It was 8-bit with only 64KB of memory. Then I
>          spent two years to write these four programs as well as unpublished
>          Midi-Workstation (8-track real-time Midi recorder) all by machine
>          code using SuperMon without assembler so that it runs fast and leave
>          enough memory for music data. Each of these programs was sold for
>          $49.95 in retail stores furnished with handsome package came with
>          5-inch floppy disk and manual. In 15 years, now we can buy 1GHz PC
>          for the same price as I bought my first C64. And I wonder what kind
>          of computer we can buy 15 years from now by paying $600?" (RK)
>          Sadly, Ryo Kawasaki passed away on April 13th, 2020 at the age of 73.
>
> COMMENT: "Satellite Station was created &amp; recorded in 1983 and used as one of
>          demo songs with Rhythm Rocker." (RK)

>          Armalyte." (KH)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/O/Omoroca/
> COMMENT: Kim Homann's own comments are denoted (KH).

>          "Just Good Friends". It was "covered" in 1996 by Dieter Bohlen, who
>          we had sent a demo tape to, and released in 1996 as Blue System's
>          single "Only With You" and on his album "Body To Body". Of course I
>          was neither asked for permission nor given a profit sharing. The
>          single went #58 on the German charts." (KH)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/O/Omoroca/
> COMMENT: Kim Homann's own comments are denoted (KH).
>
> COMMENT: "I wrote this as a love song for a girl named Kim, at least I thought
>          so. She of course never listened to it, as she didn't want to have
>          anything to do with a freaky C64 nerd like me. Weeks later I found
>          out that the girl's name wasn't Kim at all, but I didn't change the
>          title of this tune to her real name. Mind the funny ping-pong sound
>          starting at 3:09! ;-)" (KH)

## Medusa

Path: `MUSICIANS/O/Omoroca/Medusa.sid`

Author: Kim Homann (omoroca) · Release/year: 1989 Kim Homann

RSID; BASIC flag clear; 1 subtunes. Complete-file MD5: `53c9ab7c5190de9eccaa592b42501a9b`; SHA-256: `10c9fed77c6a578f6a96aecd89176fb65dafdc9c4146cc72883918a2d78f3774`.

| Subtune | Seconds | Evidence | Reference RMS; writes | Neither | K-only | B-only |
|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 10 | kernal-sensitive | 5.389e-2; 69778 | ΔRMS 1.746e-2; writes 69778; prefix 165; drift 54; derived | identical PCM; writes 69778; prefix 69778; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 1.746e-2; writes 69778; prefix 165; drift 54 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/O/Omoroca/
> COMMENT: Kim Homann's own comments are denoted (KH).
>
> COMMENT: "My first tune with digidrums, which I had ripped from the game
>          "Danger Freak". This tune marks a turning-point in my personal
>          development as a musician, putting the focus more on harmonies and
>          elaborate arpeggios than trying to produce a "cool sound". From this
>          tune on, I also made myself a strict rule that I FIRST finish
>          composing the tune on my piano or my Casio keyboard and THEN switch
>          the C128 on and start with the data input. In order to reflect this,
>          I also started to give the tunes names instead of numbers again." (KH)

>
> (#3)
>    NAME: Intro Tune
> COMMENT: The tune falls out of synch at around 4:50, which is when the tune
>          should loop. However, this is due to the music data in the game
>          behaving exactly the same way. You would never get to hear this part
>          of the tune in the game, as the intro would finish and load the first
>          level before the music got to that point.
>          "I guess I had some time pressure... I knew it would fade away
>          anyhow... never thought about that fact that people in 2000 would
>          listen to it with SIDPlay.... :)" (RO)
>          The data has been restored in this SID, to make it loop properly,
>          and is confirmed by Reyn to be correct.

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/P/PVCF/
> COMMENT: PVCF is short for "Pro Velochistische Contra Fraction".
>
> COMMENT: "Interesting fact: it's a 2 channel digi music but not a
>          Reflex-Tracker tune. It's a routine which allows you theoretical add
>          3 SID voices. The music editor is named 'Perfect Sound 3.2'.
>          Unfortunately the 2 digi voices have always the speed (and pitch) of
>          the first digi voice and if you really use both voices the SID
>          channels were nearly destroyed and the CPU was down...
>          We used this track in the note file of our (Reflex) first trackmo
>          'Cafe Odd'. Later it became a very nice practice for us to use always
>          a very special notefile-soundroutine for our trackmos.
>          All (!) samples was sampled with my Phillips-Workman!!
>          Sample-software by Zork and TTS! Sampler by Zork's Brother." (PVCF)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/P/PVCF/
> COMMENT: PVCF is short for "Pro Velochistische Contra Fraction".
>
> COMMENT: "The SID music and the digisequence was composed with two different
>          routines: 3sid voices in DMC4.x and after finishing I have loaded
>          it in Polonus' DigiTracker and added the samplesequences. Very
>          funny way to compose: if you changed a sample, for example after
>          2 min in songlength, you have to start the song from begin to check
>          if it works in minute two. I was very masochistic those days.
>          Polonus did NOT include a fast forward routine, so later I added
>          one in his editor." (PVCF)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/P/PVCF/
> COMMENT: PVCF is short for "Pro Velochistische Contra Fraction".
>
> COMMENT: "The samples were ripped from Jeroen Tel, Rob Hubbard/Simon Nicol
>          and Chris Hülsbeck if I remember right. One of my countless
>          Rockmonitor songs. I composed about 8-9 months with some versions
>          of Hülsbeck' Soundmonitor and Rockmonitors and similar composing
>          programs. I made about 30-40 songs with digis, not a single one
>          was released! In 1997 I gave this bunch of disks to Happymaker,
>          together with tons of disks with samples... Only this song and
>          Trance_HQ.sid are left, which I found later (1999) on a disc
>          which was in a 1541 drive which I plugged on my PC just to look
>          if I can use StarCommander to transfer stuff from a C64 disc to
>          a harddrive." (PVCF)
